# Team queue: a shared server where every member's Main picks up team plans
Plan file: /home/ukd/projects/neurastream/docs/plans/20261027_1305_team_queue.md
Worktree: /home/ukd/Work/neurastream-team-queue
Planner: TeamQueueTickP8ab
Branch: feat/team-queue
Summary: Teams get a queue server on their own Tailscale. Each member's Main picks up team plans with its own tokens, and nobody else can touch a plan while it is held. Plans from personal repos never leave your machine.

## Goal
- Owner (2026-10-27 ~12:10): one Tailscale per install must become many, labelled. "multiple people can see the queue of that particular tailscale instance … pick up stuff from the queue … so that everybody shares their tokens. But at the same time it should be safe … some plans in my machine are my own … PM and CP belong to my team … neurastream, gateforge belong just me."
- ~12:30 answers:
  - the queue lives on a server, and the team's Tailscale is on that server; a Tailscale on a personal machine = personal, anything else = team;
  - labels are per repository;
  - in a shared queue, anyone on the team approves plans;
  - a dead Main's plan is free after 30 min;
  - "the most important thing is to know for a fact that the plan is being worked on and … nobody else does the same work";
  - open source, so it must be done properly.
- Worker: **deepseek only** (owner ~12:30).
- Waits on: /home/ukd/projects/neurastream/docs/plans/20261027_1300_multi_main.md (ClaimStore, Main identity, claim guards).
- Waits on: /home/ukd/projects/neurastream/docs/plans/20261028_1545_main_call_budget.md (P6b(d) reads its daily call counter; INCIDENT I-20261031-16).
- Done =
  0. `neurastream ui` is renamed `neurastream workspace` everywhere (owner ~13:50), with no alias left.
  1. `neurastream workspace on --team <name>` on a server makes a running queue server reachable only on its tailnet. It has a member list, and its own page shows the team queue, holders, waiting questions and approve buttons.
  2. `neurastream team join <url>` on a member PC connects that member's Main.
  3. Plans that carry `Queue: team` in a repo labelled for that team are synced to the server. Every other plan is never sent, including unmarked plans in team repos; both the client and the server refuse them.
  4. Claims are server-side with the same rules as multi_main.
  5. Any member's "go" approves a team plan.
  6. Each Main uses its own model tokens; the server never sees provider keys.

## Background
Decisions (Main, from the owner's answers; change before spawn if he disagrees):
- **Plans live on the server for team repos.** The server is the source of truth for team plan files, versioned by content sha. A write must name the version it was based on; a stale base → 409, and the client re-reads. Members get a read copy under `<queue_state>/team/<team>/plans/`. Code still moves through the repo's normal MRs, and the server never holds code.
- **Two markers, both needed (owner ~13:20: "we might still wanna not move a team plan to the team queue … we need a marker on top of the label whether it should go to the team queue").** (1) The repo label says a repo MAY share. (2) A plan header line `Queue: team` says this plan DOES share. A plan without it stays personal, even in a team repo, so nothing is shared by accident. `neurastream team share <plan>` adds the line and uploads the plan. `team unshare <plan>` (only while you hold the claim, or the plan is unclaimed) removes it from the server and keeps your local copy. A plan whose repo has no team label is refused by `share`.
- **Repo label = the git remote URL.** The client config has `teams: [{ name, server, repos: ["gitlab.com/aetherios/*", …] }]`. A repo whose `origin` matches no team is personal. The server keeps its own `repos` allow-list and refuses a plan whose remote it does not list. Both sides must agree before anything is stored.
- **Who is who = Tailscale.** The server binds only its tailnet address. Every request is identified by `tailscale whois` of the peer (the src/core/tailscale.ts seam), and that login must be in the server's `members`. There are no passwords and no tokens to leak.
  - A member PC reaches the server either by joining the team tailnet or by having the server node **shared** into their personal tailnet (Tailscale node sharing). With sharing, whois still names the member's login.
  - "Server = team" is the rule. INCIDENT I-20261027-98 (owner ~13:40: "we dont need mypage and teamserver hardcoded like this if we param those"): one command family with a scope flag, no separate `team-server` command. `neurastream ui on --login <l>` = personal page; `neurastream ui on --team <name>` = team page + queue server; `ui off [--team <name>]`; `ui members add|remove|list <login> --team <name>`. Owner ~13:50 picked `workspace`: `neurastream workspace on --login <l>` / `workspace on --team <name>` / `workspace off [--team <name>]` / `workspace members add|remove|list <login> --team <name>`. `--team` refuses to start on a machine where the personal page is on, and the personal page never serves team data.
- **Claims** use the multi_main `ClaimStore` contract, backed by SQLite on the server (one transaction per claim, so it is atomic). The heartbeat every 60 s is an HTTP call, and a claim is free after 30 min without one. If the server is unreachable, the guards refuse spawns on team plans (fail closed), and personal plans keep working.
- **Approvals:** a WAITING line on a team plan is answered by any member, through their own phone page/chat (forwarded) or on the team page. The answer is stored with login + time and shown to everyone.
- **Audit:** every write (plan put, claim, release, approve) goes to an append-only audit log on the server with login + time. Admin = the login that ran `init`. Members are added and removed with `neurastream workspace members add|remove <login> --team <name>`, admin only.

## Forbidden
- Never send a plan, a progress entry, a WAITING line or a file path unless its repo is labelled for that team AND the plan carries `Queue: team`. The check runs in the client before the request, and a fail-before test proves it. The server checks again on its side.
- No auth other than tailnet whois + the member list, and no endpoint without it (health included). Never bind 0.0.0.0. Never shell out with request data.
- Limits: plan ≤ 256 KB, request ≤ 1 MB, 60 writes/min per login (429 above).
- Host-neutral core (docs/adapter.md). Tests inject the Tailscale seam, the clock and a temp SQLite file; they never use the real tailnet or home.
- Work only in /home/ukd/Work/neurastream-team-queue on feat/team-queue.
  - Never push, tag, publish or install.
  - Commit files by name. Never `git add -A` or `git add .`; never `--no-verify`.
- Every test run goes through `/home/ukd/Work/heavy-run.sh deepseek "<label>" -- nice -n 19 timeout 600 bun test …` with tool timeout 0.
- Read /home/ukd/projects/opus-implementer-method.md first. Commit after every function.
- Stop and report within ~100 calls. Write one dated `## Progress` bullet after the `Status:` line, ending with `Findings:`.

(1) Plan id: `:id` in every claim route is the stored plan id `teamPlanId(remote, relPath)` (team-server.ts:49, the sha256 hex P2a's PUT stores as `plans.id`), URL-decoded as `/plans/:id` is (:189-197; bad encoding → 400). The claim body carries no remote, so `POST /claims/:id` reads `plans` for (id, config.team) inside its transaction and answers 404 `{ error: "no such plan" }` when absent, storing nothing; claim rows therefore exist only for stored plans, one per plan (claims.id is the PRIMARY KEY) and are deleted on release, so nothing needs pruning. (2) Table claims (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS inside openTeamDb's schema block, below): id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, team, main, host, pid, since, beat (the `Claim` fields, src/core/claims.ts:14-25, with `plan` = id) plus `login`, the whois login that took the claim. Every response row is a `Claim` with `plan` = id plus `login` and `mine` (the one definition in (7)), including the `heldBy` of a 409 (7a) and the holder of (7d). Id vs path: `Claim.plan` is documented as the plan path (claims.ts:15); the server knows no local path, so on the wire `plan` is the id, and P3's remoteClaimStore owns the mapping both ways: path → id by `teamPlanId(<repo origin>, <repo-relative path>)` before each request, id → local path through the ids of the team plans its sync pulled, so every `Claim` a ClaimStore caller sees still has `plan` = path; a row whose id maps to no local plan is dropped from `list()`. (3) TTL: new REQUIRED option `claimTtlMin: number` on TeamServerOptions (:29-40), no default inside the server, the same rule as fileClaimStore's required `ttlMin` (claims.ts:61-66); its source is the server host's `queue.claim_ttl_min` (config.ts:118, checked >= 1 at :643; default 30 from templates/config.yml:122, which loadConfig reads as defaults, config.ts:847); P6 passes `cfg.queue.claim_ttl_min` when it starts the server; tests pass 30 (the existing helper tests/team-server.test.ts:70 gets `claimTtlMin: 30`). (4) Expiry: lift fileClaimStore's closure `expired()` (claims.ts:49-51) to one exported function in claims.ts, `claimExpired(c: { beat: number }, now: number, ttlMin: number): boolean` = `now - c.beat >= ttlMin * 60`; fileClaimStore calls it at its two uses (:100, :118) with behaviour unchanged, and the team server calls it; no second copy of the rule. (5) Write limit: beats, claims and releases are writes and count against P2a's 60 writes/min per login like PUT (Forbidden :44 has no exception). Move P2a's inline counter (:206-213) into one closure inside startTeamServer that PUT and the three claim writes call at the same point (after readBody's 413, before JSON parse); no second counter. Consequence, stated for P3/P7: one login can keep about 60 claims alive at 60 s beats; above that beats get 429 and claims lapse at the TTL. (6) Concurrency: every claim, beat and release runs in one `db.transaction(...)` called with `.immediate(...)` (BEGIN IMMEDIATE), the form P2a's writePlan uses (:151, :239); the read of the row, the expiry decision, the write and the audit row are all inside it. Refusals never throw: like writePlan, which returns `{ status: 409 }` at :156-157 so that only its success path reaches the audit insert at :161, the claim transaction functions return `{ status, … }` for every outcome, and each refusal branch (409, 403) runs its `refused …` audit insert and then returns, so that row commits; a throw would roll it back. Body validation (400) runs before the transaction. One shared open helper, because two processes open the same file: export `openTeamDb(dbPath: string): Database` from team-server.ts = the `mkdirSync` (:112), `new Database` (:113), then `PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000`, then the schema exec (:114-135, plus the claims table), so concurrent schema creation and later writes from a second connection wait instead of failing at once with SQLITE_BUSY; startTeamServer calls it in place of :112-135 (members seeding stays in startTeamServer at :136-137, unchanged in P2b: no member can be removed before P2c adds removal, and P2c makes the seed run once per database, see P2c), and P2c's exported members functions, which P6's `workspace members` CLI calls in its own process, open the file only through it; no other `new Database` on this file. (7) Routes, all behind P2a's auth chain (:175-186, Origin included on POST/DELETE) and dispatched before the 404 fallback at :203. Body for POST /claims/:id: `{ main, host, pid }` with main matching `MAIN_ID_RE` (exported, src/core/guards.ts:98), host a string of 1-253 chars, pid a positive integer, else 400; body for beat and DELETE: `{ main }` with the same main check, else 400. Login is always the whois login from the auth chain, never a body field. Trust model, stated for P7: `main`, `host` and `pid` are client-supplied and unverified labels inside a login; only the login is authenticated, so the boundary between members is the login and two Mains of one member are told apart only by their honest `main`. Decision table, `now` = the injected clock. One definition of "mine", used here and by the (7e) field: "mine" = row.login equals the caller's whois login, nothing else. "Same main" = row.main equals body main, a separate condition; "own" = mine AND same main. (7a) `POST /claims/:id`: plan absent → 404; no claim row → insert (since = beat = now, login = caller) → 200 Claim; row own (live or expired) → idempotent: `since` kept, `beat` = now, host/pid unchanged → 200 Claim (deviation from fileClaimStore, which matches main only at claims.ts:94: P2b requires mine AND same main); row not own and live → 409 `{ heldBy: Claim }`, row unchanged — this includes a mine row with a different main (two Mains of one member are two Mains, as the file store's 409 at :100) and a not-mine row with the same main string; row not own and expired → takeover: row replaced (since = beat = now, login = caller) → 200 Claim. Takeover happens only here. (7b) `POST /claims/:id/beat`: no row → 404 (file heartbeat returns false, claims.ts:110); row own → `beat` = now → 200 Claim, also when the row is expired but not yet taken over (the file store does the same, claims.ts:106-113; the claim is still this Main's until someone else's POST takes it); row not own → 403, row unchanged. (7c) `DELETE /claims/:id`: no row → 404, nothing audited (file release is a no-op, claims.ts:125; P3 maps 404 to the void `release()`); row own (live or expired) → row deleted → 200; row not own → 403, row unchanged (the file store's main-only match at :125 is NOT copied). `--force`: the server has no force path. `neurastream claim release --force` (src/cli/claim.ts:56-73) sends the holder's own main (`store.release(file, h.main)`, :72), so on a team plan it frees a claim held by another Main of the caller's own login (the row is mine and the sent main is the row's, so it is own) and gets 403 for a claim held by any other login; another member's claim ends only at the TTL or by P2c's member removal. P3 owns the CLI message for that 403. (7d) `GET /claims/:id` (holder, claims.ts:35): plan absent → 404; no row or expired row → 200 `{ claim: null }`; live row → 200 `{ claim: Claim }` with `login` and `mine` (P3's holder checks need it: mainId defaults to `main` on every PC, so `main` alone cannot tell this member's Main from another member's). (7e) `GET /claims` (list, claims.ts:38): 200 `{ claims: Claim[] }`, every row of config.team including expired ones, exactly as fileClaimStore.list() (:128-137) returns expired files, each row carrying `login`, `main` and `mine` (the one definition above: row.login equals the caller's whois login; `main` stays its own field and the caller compares it). `mine` exists for the omp heartbeat loop (src/adapters/omp/index.ts:962-964), which beats every listed claim whose `main` equals this Main's id; mainId defaults to the same string on every PC, so without a login filter it would beat other members' rows each minute (403, a `refused` audit row and one of the caller's 60 writes each); P3 owns the loop change (see P3). (8) Audit (P2a's insert-only audit table, :128-134, one row inside the same transaction): successful claim (new, takeover and idempotent re-claim) route `POST /claims/<id>`; successful release route `DELETE /claims/<id>`; every refused steal: 409 on POST route `refused POST /claims/<id>`, 403 on beat route `refused POST /claims/<id>/beat`, 403 on DELETE route `refused DELETE /claims/<id>`. Beats that succeed are NOT audited (one per held plan per minute would bury the log; Background :39 lists put, claim, release and approve, not beats). 400, 404 and 429 answers are not audited, as P2a audits no refused PUT. P2a's "every write appends an audit row" (:62) reads as "every stored plan write"; the claim scope is this list. (9) Not P2b: removing a member's claims. P2c's `DELETE /members/:login` deletes that login's claim rows in the same transaction (see P2c); until then a removed member's claims lapse at the TTL because P2a's whois∈members check already refuses their beats. Tests (tests/team-server-claims.test.ts; injected whois/peer/clock, temp SQLite, port 0, PUT a team plan first through P2a's route so the id exists): POST on an id with no stored plan → 404 and no claims row; A claims → 200; A re-claims after 10 min → 200, same `since`, new `beat`; A's login with main `b` → 409 heldBy A; B's beat on A's claim → 403 and A's `beat` unchanged; B's DELETE → 403 and `GET /claims/:id` still returns A; B's POST on A's live claim → 409 heldBy A; one `refused …` audit row for each of the four refusals the shipped tests check at 0d26468 (read after the responses, so a throwing refusal branch fails the test): B's POST on A's live claim → `refused POST /claims/<id>`, B's beat and A's beat with main `m2` → two `refused POST /claims/<id>/beat`, B's DELETE → `refused DELETE /claims/<id>` (A's own re-claim with another main goes through the same audited 409 branch, team-server.ts:247-249 at 0d26468, but its test checks only the 409, `heldBy` and the unchanged row); A's beat 200 and no audit row; beat at TTL+1 s before anyone else claims → 200 (row revived); after the TTL B's POST wins, A's beat → 403, A's DELETE → 403; `GET /claims/:id` → null for no row and for an expired row, 404 for an unknown plan; as B, `GET /claims/:id` on A's live claim shows `mine: false` and the 409 `heldBy` too, as A `mine: true`; DELETE and beat on no row → 404; `GET /claims` lists an expired row, and as B shows A's row `mine: false` and B's own `mine: true`, and as A shows A's row `mine: true` (mine does not look at main); bad main (`Main!`) → 400; claim/beat/release share the 60/min budget with PUT (61st write → 429); one audit row per successful claim and release; `openTeamDb(path)` → `PRAGMA busy_timeout` reads 5000; tests/claims.test.ts stays green after the claimExpired lift. Race test that really exercises BEGIN IMMEDIATE (one bun:sqlite connection is synchronous, so in-process requests are serialized and cannot tell IMMEDIATE from a deferred BEGIN): a child script that tests/team-server-claims.test.ts writes into its temp dir at run time, as claims.test.ts's writeChild does (:43-53; no new tracked file: team-queue-scope.sh:11 allows only `tests/*.test.ts` under tests/), runs `startTeamServer` in its own `Bun.spawn` process (loopback hostname, port 0, the shared temp dbPath from argv and `claimTtlMin: 30`; as shipped at 0d26468 members = the four test logins `m1`..`m4`; the server code gets no test hook; the child prints its port), the multi_main race pattern (tests/claims.test.ts:41-57, :165). The child's stubs, all in the child script: tailscale `ip4()` returns the fixed tailnet address `100.64.1.2` and `dnsName()` the test's DNS name, so hostAllowlist (owner-ui.ts:1792, built at team-server.ts:111, checked at :175) accepts Host `100.64.1.2:<child port>`; `whois` maps `100.64.2.<k>` to login `m<k>` (k = 1..4), else null; `logins()` the four logins; `state()` "running"; `peerOf` returns the request's `X-Test-Peer` header. The test sends each request with Host `100.64.1.2:<port>`, Origin `http://100.64.1.2:<port>` and `X-Test-Peer: 100.64.2.<k>` for login `m<k>`. Four children on one dbPath (started together, so the schema exec also races; openTeamDb's busy_timeout must hold), one team plan PUT through child 1 per round, 20 concurrent `POST /claims/:id` spread across them from the four logins with 20 distinct Mains `m0`..`m19`, five rounds on fresh ids: exactly one 200 and nineteen 409 (so zero 500) each round, and one claims row. At 0d26468 (P2b's commit, P2c's base) the code moved: teamPlanId :49 → :53, the open + schema block :112-135 → `openTeamDb` :155-192, the members seed :136-137 → :204-205, the 404 fallback :203 → :378. - [x] P2c Team routes. All in src/core/team-server.ts unless named; no new module; tests in the new tests/team-server-routes.test.ts (the Done check names it). Code this phase builds on, cites at 0d26468: `openTeamDb` (team-server.ts:155-192: mkdir, `new Database`, `PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000`, one CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS exec for plans, members, audit, claims; there is no `meta` table yet, this phase adds it); the members seed inside startTeamServer (:204-205, `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO members` for every `config.members` login on each start); `isMember` (:206, read on every request); the insert-only `insertAudit` (:218; columns login, time, route, plan_id); `planExists` (:232); the `.immediate` transaction functions that return a status for every outcome so refusal audit rows commit (claimOf/beatOf/releaseOf :239-276); the write counter `takeWriteSlot` (:277-284); `readBody` (:74-84) and `jsonObject` (:143-152); the claim route's write order (:299-306: readBody → 413 at :300, takeWriteSlot → 429 at :302, jsonObject → 400 at :304); the auth chain in the fetch handler (:337-348: Host, peer, whois login :343, member :345, Origin on PUT/POST/DELETE :346); the `/claims/:id` decode (:368-375, bad encoding → 400); the 404 fallback (:378). `TeamServerConfig` (:23-29) already carries `admin` and `leader`. (1) Schema, added to openTeamDb's exec: `meta (key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, value TEXT NOT NULL)`; `approvals (plan_id TEXT NOT NULL, login TEXT NOT NULL, time INTEGER NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (plan_id, login))`; `votes (login TEXT PRIMARY KEY, plan_ids TEXT NOT NULL, time INTEGER NOT NULL)` (plan_ids = JSON array, the caller's preferred order); `paused (login TEXT PRIMARY KEY, time INTEGER NOT NULL)`. The leader's override is the meta row `order_override`, value = JSON `{ plan_ids, login, time }`, replaced on each override (one value, so no table of its own). (2) Start-time members rule, so a removed member stays removed and the admin is always a member: today's seed (:204-205) would bring back a login removed by `DELETE /members/:login` or `workspace members remove` while it is still in the server config. Rule: the seed becomes one `.immediate` transaction run by startTeamServer only (not by the exported functions of (5)) with two steps. Step A, seed once: only when the meta row `members_seeded` is absent, insert every `config.members` login and then the meta row; when the row is present, step A does nothing. Step B, admin upsert, on EVERY start: `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO members (login) VALUES (config.admin)`. Why step B: the admin is server config (P6 changes it by editing the server config and restarting the unit), and step A runs once, so without step B a new admin named after the first start would never be a member and every request of the only login that can manage the team would be 403. Step B cannot resurrect a removed login against the admin's will: it only ever inserts the current `config.admin`, and (4c) refuses to remove `config.admin`. Step B never removes anyone: after an admin change the old admin stays an ordinary member (403 on admin-only routes) until the new admin removes it. Neither step is audited (no request, no login acting). Not chosen: refusing to start when config.admin is not a member (recovery would need a members add before the server can start, for no safety gain over step B). Config `members` is therefore the initial list only; after the first start the members table changes only through add/remove and step B (P6 prints this, P7 documents it). Not "seed when the table is empty" (removing the last member and restarting would re-seed everyone) and not a tombstone table (re-adding a removed login would need a second rule). (3) Roles, checked with the chain's whois login (:343) and nothing from the body: "admin only" = login equals `config.admin` (the login that ran init), else 403 `{ error: "forbidden: admin only" }`; "leader only" = login equals `config.leader`, else 403 `{ error: "forbidden: leader only" }`; "any member" = the auth chain alone. Where the check sits is fixed by (4)'s order: on a write it runs after takeWriteSlot, so a refused role check spends one of the caller's 60 writes/min like any other write (Forbidden :44 has no exception; P2b (5)); a refused role check on a write stores nothing and inserts one audit row (a single insertAudit run, plan_id null) whose route is `refused <route>` with the route string of (4) — except `POST /members`, whose refused route is `refused POST /members` with no login: the body of a refused call is read only for its size and never parsed, so the requested login is not known (the success route stays `POST /members/<login>`). A refused `GET /audit` is a read: no body, no write slot, not audited. (4) Routes, each dispatched before the 404 fallback (:378); a path segment (`:id`, `:login`) is URL-decoded at dispatch, after the auth chain and before readBody, the write slot and the role check — the `/claims/:id` order, whose decode (:368-375) runs after the member check (:345) and the Origin check (:346) — so bad encoding → 400, not audited, no write slot spent; a stranger with a bad segment therefore gets the chain's 403, and a non-admin with a bad `:login` gets 400, not 403 (a refused row needs the decoded route string). Every POST/DELETE below runs in this order, the claim routes' order with the role check added after the write slot: auth chain (:337-348) → path-segment decode (400) → readBody (413) → takeWriteSlot (429) → role check (403, audited `refused …`) → jsonObject (400) → field checks (400) → one `.immediate` transaction that reads, decides, writes and inserts its audit row (route string as named, plan_id null unless named), returning a status for every outcome like claimOf. Body: every POST/DELETE below must be a JSON object; where no field is named the body is `{}` (any object is accepted and its fields ignored), and an empty or non-object body → 400 `{ error: "body must be a JSON object" }` (jsonObject returns null for an empty body, since JSON.parse("") throws). This holds for `DELETE /members/:login`, `POST /approve/:id`, `POST /pause` and `POST /resume`; P4/P5 clients send `{}`. Audit rule for P2c's routes: audited = every successful write (its route string), every refused role check on a write ((3), `refused <route>`) and the admin self-removal 409 of (4c) (`refused DELETE /members/<admin>`), i.e. every refusal that passed the write slot and decides something about the team; not audited = the auth chain's 403s, 413 and 429 (they come before or at the write slot), and every 400 and 404 (bad path encoding, body, field checks, no such plan, no such member: nothing is decided and nothing changes). (4a) `POST /approve/:id`, any member: `:id` = the stored plan id; body `{}` (a body `login` field is ignored); plan absent for config.team (planExists) → 404 `{ error: "no such plan" }`; else `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO approvals (plan_id, login, time)` with the caller's login and now (a repeat keeps the first time) → 200 `{ approval: { plan, login, time } }` from the stored row; audit route `POST /approve/<id>`, plan_id = id, also on a repeat. P6b(e)'s refusal of the holder's own approve is not P2c. (4b) `POST /members`, admin only: body `{ login }`, a string of 1-256 chars with no whitespace or control character, else 400; `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO members` → 200 `{ login, added: true|false }`; audit route `POST /members/<login>`; refused route `refused POST /members` (see (3)). (4c) `DELETE /members/:login`, admin only: `:login` URL-decoded at dispatch; body `{}`. After the role check (so a non-admin's `DELETE /members/<admin>` is 403 with a `refused DELETE /members/<admin>` row), as the first step of the shared remove transaction of (5): `:login` = `config.admin` → 409 `{ error: "the admin login cannot be removed" }`, nothing changed except one audit row `refused DELETE /members/<admin>` (acting login, plan_id null; `removeTeamMember` writes the same row with acting login `config.admin`), so the team never loses the only login that can manage it; changing the admin is a server-config change on the server host (P6) that step B of (2) applies at the next start. Login not in members → 404 `{ error: "no such member" }`, not audited. Else the same transaction deletes the members row and that login's rows in claims (`WHERE login = ? AND team = config.team`), votes and paused, and audits route `DELETE /members/<login>` → 200 `{ removed: <login>, claimsFreed: <deleted claim rows> }`. Approvals stay (history). The freed plans are claimable at once; the removed login's next request is 403 at the member check (:345). Removing the leader login is allowed; `/order/override` then answers 403 to everyone until the config names a new leader. (4d) `GET /audit`, admin only (role check right after the auth chain; no body, no write slot): 200 `{ audit: [{ id, login, time, route, plan_id }] }`, the newest 200 rows, newest first (`ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 200`); P5's audit tail. (4e) `POST /order`, any member: body `{ plan_ids: string[] }`, 1-500 entries, no duplicates, each a stored plan id of config.team (planExists), else 400 naming the first bad entry; `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO votes` for the caller's login with now → 200 `{ vote: { login, plan_ids, time } }`; audit route `POST /order`. (4f) `POST /order/override`, leader only: same body and checks as (4e); replaces the meta row `order_override` → 200 `{ override: { plan_ids, login, time } }`; audit route `POST /order/override`. How votes and the override become one team order is P6b(b)'s rule; P2c only stores them. (4g) `POST /pause` and `POST /resume`, any member, acting on the caller's own login only (body `{}`, no login field read): pause = `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO paused (login, time)` → 200 `{ paused: true }`; resume = delete the caller's row → 200 `{ paused: false }`, also when no row existed; audit routes `POST /pause` and `POST /resume`. The server does not refuse a paused login's claims; P6b(c) stops the client and releases its claims. Read routes for approvals, votes, the override and paused are not P2c: P4 and P5 add the GET routes they render from. (5) Transport for P6's CLI: export `listTeamMembers(dbPath: string): string[]`, `addTeamMember(dbPath: string, config: TeamServerConfig, login: string): { ok: true; added: boolean } | { ok: false; error: string }` and `removeTeamMember(dbPath: string, config: TeamServerConfig, login: string): { ok: true; claimsFreed: number } | { ok: false; error: string }`. Each opens the file only through `openTeamDb` (no second open path), closes it before returning, and runs the same add/remove transaction the HTTP route runs (one module-level function per write taking the Database, the config, the acting login and now, called by both the route and the export; no copy), with acting login = `config.admin` (the CLI runs on the server host, the admin's machine) and the same audit route strings. No write slot (the counter lives in the running server; the CLI is local to the server host) and no role check (acting login is the admin). They refuse exactly as the routes do (the admin login → the 409 text, a login not in members → the 404 text, a bad login string → the 400 text) and, while the meta row `members_seeded` is absent, with "team <name> not initialised: its server has never started on this database; start it once, then retry" (P6 installs the unit and does not promise a start, so the text names no command), so a remove before the first start cannot be undone by the first seed. They never run step A or step B of (2). P6's `workspace members add|remove|list --team` CLI calls them on the server host (no HTTP); member PCs use only the HTTP routes. Tests (tests/team-server-routes.test.ts; injected whois/peer/clock, temp SQLite, port 0, a start helper like tests/team-server.test.ts:68-82 with `claimTtlMin: 30`; config admin A, leader B, members A, B, C; PUT a team plan first through P2a's route where a route needs a stored id; every POST/DELETE that names no field sends body `{}`; "as X" = whois answers X for the request): a stranger login → 403 on each new route; as B (non-admin) → 403 on `POST /members` (body `{ "login": "D" }`), `DELETE /members/C` and `GET /audit`, with exactly one `refused POST /members` row (no login in the route) and one `refused DELETE /members/C` row, and none for `GET /audit`; as B, `DELETE /members/A` → 403 with one `refused DELETE /members/A` row (role check before the admin check); decode order: as B, `DELETE /members/%E0%A4%A` → 400 (not 403) and no audit row, and as a stranger the same request → 403 (auth chain first); as A (non-leader) → 403 on `POST /order/override` with one `refused POST /order/override` row; refused role checks spend the write budget: with A's budget empty (no earlier write by A in the last 60 s of the injected clock; do the setup PUT as C), A sends 61 `POST /order/override` within one minute → 60 × 403 and 60 `refused POST /order/override` rows, the 61st → 429 and still 60 rows (fail-before: with the role check before takeWriteSlot the 61st answers 403 and writes a 61st row); - [x] P3 Client store and team sync. New file src/core/team-client.ts (absent at 01c0ce8) holds `remoteClaimStore(server)`, its request entry, the chooser, the summary holder and the team sync below; the only other new code is the `team sync` subcommand in the existing src/cli/team.ts. `remoteClaimStore` implements `ClaimStore` (src/core/claims.ts:32-39) over HTTP. Interface widening, so the remote store can implement it: `ClaimStore` becomes generic over its outage value, one interface, `ClaimStore<U = Unreachable>`, with `claim(plan, main): Claim | Held | U` (claims.ts:33), `holder(plan): Claim | U | null` (:35) and `list(): Claim[] | U` (:38); `heartbeat` stays `boolean` and `release` stays `void`. fileClaimStore's declared return type (claims.ts:71) becomes `ClaimStore<never>`, so its answers keep today's exact types (`Claim | Held | never` = `Claim | Held`): every direct fileClaimStore user (the heartbeat's file list, tests/claims*.test.ts, tests/guards-claims.test.ts, tests/team-labels.test.ts) compiles unchanged, and the compiler proves the file store never answers Unreachable; remoteClaimStore and the chooser return `ClaimStore` (U = Unreachable). The interface is synchronous, and so is every guard that calls it (claimGate guards.ts:1848 inside checkSpawn :1870, the Main edit gate :2149-2150, checkInject :1960 through queueSummary at :1967), so the store keeps the interface and makes each request synchronously: `spawnSync(process.execPath, [<request entry>])` with the JSON request on stdin (no shell, argv carries no request data); the entry path is resolved from team-client.ts's own `import.meta.url`, never from the cwd or config. Making ClaimStore and every guard async instead would touch every guard entry and the adapter hooks; not chosen. Blocking bound, scoped per process: the spawnSync timeout is 5 s, so one request blocks the calling process up to 5 s. A remote store that got no answer returns Unreachable (below) for the next 60 s (injected clock) without sending a request, and the chooser keeps one remote store per `cfg.teams` entry in a module-level map, so that memo lives exactly as long as one process. Inside the Main process (the omp adapter imports the guards and runs its hooks and heartbeat in-process, adapters/omp/index.ts:32 and :961-966) every hook and tick shares the memo: while a team server is down, Main blocks at most 5 s per down team server per 60 s. Every CLI process starts with an empty memo: each `neurastream guard <event>` call (the hook path of any other harness), `neurastream claim`, `queue-check`, `team unshare` and `team sync` pays up to 5 s per down team server it asks (once per team per process; later requests in that process get the memo's Unreachable at once). Not chosen: a memo file under queue_state shared across processes (one more state file and lock to shave a 5 s wait that only happens while a server is down). Requests carry no token or secret; every write request carries `Origin: http://<server host:port>` (P2a's Origin check, CSRF hygiene; whois stays the auth). Fail closed — an unreachable server is a value, never null: claims.ts gains `export interface Unreachable { unreachable: string }` (the server URL). The remote store returns it from `holder()`, `claim()` and `list()` on a timeout, a refused connection, a 5xx or an unparseable answer; null stays "free" exactly as holder()'s doc says (claims.ts:34-35, the file store's :119); `heartbeat()` returns false and `release()` stays void (nothing more is sent; the claim lapses at the TTL). Not chosen: throwing, because checkInject's catch (guards.ts:1966-1970) would turn one down team server into "Queue unreadable" for the whole summary, personal plans included, and a site that forgets a catch fails however the hook runner treats an exception. What the compiler covers, exactly: with U = Unreachable, `bun run typecheck` fails at every site that reads a Claim field (`.main`, `.since`, `.plan`) of a holder() or claim() answer, or iterates a list() answer, before narrowing `"unreachable" in x` out — the edit gate (guards.ts:2150), `claim next` (cli/claim.ts:34, and :37 reads `c.since`), `claim list` (:46-48), `claim release` (:68), `team unshare` (cli/team.ts:79), queueSummary (lib.ts:1259-1260), queueRows (owner-ui.ts:265) and the heartbeat (adapters/omp/index.ts:964). It does NOT cover a site that only tests `"heldBy" in c` and then reads no field of the success value: claimGate (guards.ts:1854-1863) is that site — after `"heldBy" in c` it runs `fresh.push(f)` and `return null`, so an Unreachable from claim() compiles and would fall through to an allowed spawn. Rule for claimGate, required: an explicit `if ("unreachable" in c)` branch placed BEFORE the `"heldBy" in c` test, which releases the claims this call took and returns heldProblem's unreachable text, so the spawn is refused; and its holder(f) answer is checked the same way before `heldBefore` is computed (through heldByOther, below). Fresh claims are released through the store that took each one: today claimGate holds one store for every file (guards.ts:1849) and releases through it (:1858), but with the chooser each file has its own store, and a fresh team claim released through the file store is a silent no-op that leaves the claim on the server until its TTL — so `fresh` carries the store beside each file, and both release sites (the new unreachable branch and the `"heldBy" in c` refusal, today :1858) release each entry through its own store. The mechanism that keeps that branch, stated exactly: the claimGate outage test below (spawn refused, no claim left behind) and its fail-before (with that branch deleted, the test must fail; run once by the implementer and quoted in Progress, not committed) — not the compiler; no return-type trick is added to force a field read of the success value. Every holder site and its answer to Unreachable: (i) the Main edit gate (guards.ts:2149-2150) restores the file with heldProblem's unreachable text; (ii) claimGate (:1853-1861) as above; (iii) checkInject (:1967) and queue-check (queue-check.ts:18-21), through queueSummary (lib.ts:1259-1260) and the summary holder below: that plan's flag reads "team server <url> unreachable", every other plan as today (no throw, so no "Queue unreadable"); (iv) queueRows (owner-ui.ts:263-265): the holder chip reads "team server unreachable" in place of `Main <id>` (queueHtml :589); (v) `claim next` (cli/claim.ts:33-36): skips the plan when its holder or its claim() answer is Unreachable (an explicit `"unreachable" in c` test before `"heldBy" in c` at :36), and its exit-1 text (:40) adds "or their team server is unreachable"; (vi) `claim list` (:46-48): prints "claim: team server <url> unreachable" for each team whose list() is Unreachable and lists the rest; (vii) `claim release` (:63-68): prints "claim: team server <url> unreachable", exits 1, releases nothing; (viii) `team unshare` (cli/team.ts:76-79): prints "team unshare: team server <url> unreachable", exits 1, header unchanged; (ix) the heartbeat (adapters/omp/index.ts:962-964): that team is skipped for the tick and the file beats still run; (x) the team sync push: no PUT; (xi) queue-close's release (queue-close.ts:324): nothing is sent, CLOSED still prints, the claim lapses at the TTL. heldProblem (guards.ts:1839-1841) takes `Claim | Unreachable`: Unreachable → "team server <url> unreachable; plan <file> is refused until it answers"; `mine === false` → "plan <file> is held by another member's Main <main> since HH:MM"; otherwise today's "plan <file> is held by Main <main> since HH:MM" (a file claim, or a claim of the caller's own login on another main). Plan id mapping (P2b (2)): the server's `plan` is the plan id. Path → id: the team sync record's id for that path (below) when the path is recorded, else `teamPlanId(<repo origin>, <repo-relative path>)` (team-server.ts:53). Id → path on the way back: the record's path for that id, so every returned `Claim.plan` is a local path as claims.ts:15 documents; ids with no recorded path are dropped from `list()`. `Claim` (claims.ts:14-25) gains one optional field `mine?: boolean`, set only by the remote store from P2b's `GET /claims`, `GET /claims/:id` and 409 `heldBy` rows (wireClaim, team-server.ts:143-154: the row's login equals the caller's whois login; absent on file claims, which are this machine's own). One chooser `claimStoreFor(plan, cfg, text?)`, routing rule in this order: (1) header: it reads the plan's `Queue:` header with headerField (src/core/queue/lib.ts:349) from `text` when given, else from the file (absent or unreadable → no header); a `Queue: team` plan goes to `repoTeam(planRepo(plan), cfg)`, the order planShare already uses (team-labels.ts:114-117), and to that team's remote store when a team matches; (2) the record rule: a plan without `Queue: team` (or whose repo now matches no team) whose path is in some team's sync record goes to that team's remote store anyway — so a team plan whose header was stripped on disk outside the edit gate (a git checkout, an editor outside the harness) is still claimed and held on the server at spawn, and claimGate/checkSpawn (guards.ts:1848-1862, which read only the claim) cannot hand it to the file store while another member holds it; the record is a local JSON read, no git, and a request for such a plan carries only the id the server already stores, never text or a path, so Forbidden :42 holds; (3) else fileClaimStore (claims.ts:71). Consequence, stated as the rule for a team repo's plan with no `Queue: team` on disk: it is a team plan exactly when the sync record lists it (it was shared and synced, and not unshared since); otherwise it is personal by the owner's two-marker rule (Background :32: "a plan without it stays personal, even in a team repo"), checkSpawn does not refuse it and nothing is sent. INDEX.md, every other non-plan path and every personal plan therefore never run `git -C <dir> remote get-url origin` (team-labels.ts:100-101). The edit gate passes `e.before` (the file on disk already holds the edit, so a team plan stays guarded while an edit removes its header; the record rule covers it too). The chooser replaces the six fileClaimStore constructions: guards.ts:1834-1836 (`claims(cfg)` is removed; claimGate and the edit gate call the chooser per file, checkInject passes the summary holder), cli/claim.ts:23 (next and release per plan; list = the file store's list plus each team's remote list), owner-ui.ts:263 (the summary holder), queue-check.ts:18 (the summary holder, as checkInject), queue-close.ts:324, and the `team unshare` holder check cli/team.ts:76 (P1's local-only read; through the chooser it sees a holder on the server, so unshare of a plan another member holds is refused; `opts.store` stays the test seam). Summary holder, one GET /claims per team per summary: queueSummary calls holder() once per listed plan (lib.ts:1259; up to SUMMARY_CAP = 10 per Main prompt, every open plan for queue-check's `cap = Infinity`), and a per-plan remote holder() would be one child process and one request per team plan per prompt. So team-client.ts exports `summaryHolder(cfg): Pick<ClaimStore, "holder">`, built once per summary by checkInject (guards.ts:1967), queue-check (queue-check.ts:18) and queueRows (owner-ui.ts:263): a plan the chooser sends to the file store is answered by fileClaimStore's holder(); for a plan of team T it fetches that team's claims once, on first use within that summary (one `GET /claims`, one child, kept id-keyed — the id→path mapping `list()` applies is not run, so no row is dropped), and answers by plan ID, never by recorded path: each listed plan's id is computed exactly as the chooser maps it (the record's id for that path when recorded, else `teamPlanId(<repo origin>, <repo-relative path>)`, team-server.ts:53 — the same origin the chooser already read to pick the team), and the answer is the fetched row whose id equals it with `claimExpired(row, now, cfg.queue.claim_ttl_min)` (claims.ts:49) false, else null; an Unreachable fetch makes every plan of T answer Unreachable. Matching by recorded path instead would show a never-synced `Queue: team` plan as "free" — its id has no recorded path on this machine, so the path-mapped `list()` drops its row — while another member holds it on the server; matching by id shows that holder. Its liveness uses this member's `queue.claim_ttl_min`, not the server's `claimTtlMin`; it only colours the summary line and the chip, while every decision (claimGate, the edit gate, `claim next`/`release`, `team unshare`) still asks the server per plan through holder()/claim(). queueSummary's parameter type narrows from ClaimStore to `Pick<ClaimStore, "holder">`, the only method it calls (:1259), at all four places that name it: the signature lib.ts:1230, `let claimStore` :1237, the `typeof … .holder === 'function'` test :1239 and the cast :1240. Holder checks are login-aware: five sites decide "this Main holds it" by `main` alone — the Main edit gate (guards.ts:2150), claimGate's `heldBefore` (:1853), `claim next` (cli/claim.ts:34), `claim release` (cli/claim.ts:68, `h.main !== me && !force`) and `team unshare` (cli/team.ts:79); mainId defaults to `main` on every PC (guards.ts:101-104), so through the chooser another member's Main on the default id would pass them on a team plan. One exported `heldByOther(h: Claim | Unreachable | null, me: string): boolean` in claims.ts = `!!h && ("unreachable" in h || h.main !== me || h.mine === false)` replaces those five comparisons (claimGate: `heldBefore` = `h !== null && !heldByOther(h, mine)`, after its Unreachable check); no per-site copy. Sites that keep the main-only comparison: `claim list`'s liveness filter (cli/claim.ts:48, `store.holder(c.plan)?.main === c.main` compares a listed row with its own live holder, not with this Main; it only drops a row whose holder is Unreachable), release's `store.release(file, h.main)` and its print (:72-73), the heartbeat filter (:964, main plus `mine`, below) and the file store's own compares (claims.ts:95, :111, :126). A file claim has no `mine`, so personal plans behave as today. The server's claim writes already refuse another login's holder (P2b (7a) 409, (7b)/(7c) 403); this closes the paths that only read the holder (edit, unshare, next's skip, release). The heartbeat at adapters/omp/index.ts:961-966 has no plan to choose by, so it does not call the chooser: each tick it builds the file store, whose `list()` holds only personal claims (the chooser never sends a team plan to it), and takes the chooser's remote store for each `cfg.teams` entry, whose `list()` is that team's `GET /claims` with ids mapped to local paths; it concatenates the lists and beats each claim through the store that listed it; no union store is added. A team whose list() is Unreachable is skipped for that tick (its claims lapse at the TTL, as the fail-closed rule expects) and the file beats still run. Its filter `claim.main === id` at :964 becomes `claim.main === id && claim.mine !== false`, so other members' rows with the same main string are never beaten. `claim release` (cli/claim.ts:56-73): `heldByOther(h, me) && !force` → today's refusal (:69); `--force` with `h.mine === false` prints "claim: <plan> is held by another member's Main; it frees at the TTL or when the admin removes that member" and exits 1 with no request sent (the server would answer 403, P2b (7c)); `--force` on a claim of the caller's own login (any of its Mains) releases it as today (:72). Team sync (no sync code exists at 01c0ce8: the server's `PUT /plans/:id` route at team-server.ts:625 is the only hit; P4-P8 do not own it — P6b(a) only gates "before `team share` uploads", — so P3 owns it, in src/core/team-client.ts): `syncTeam(team, cfg)`, every request through the same request entry. Record: `<queue_state>/team/<team>/record.json` = `{ <id>: { path, sha } }` (path = the local plan path: the repo path for a pushed plan, the read copy for a pulled one), written by tmp + rename after each successful GET or PUT, and EVERY record read-modify-write — the sync's pull and push, `team share`'s one-plan push, `team unshare`'s entry delete — runs under the same file lock fileClaimStore already uses: `lock` from src/core/file-lock.ts (imported at claims.ts:11), with fileClaimStore's run() convention (claims.ts:73-83: one lock file beside the data — `<queue_state>/team/<team>/record.lock` — taken before the read, tmp + rename inside, released in `finally`). Unlocked, two concurrent processes (two terminals, or a cron run plus a manual run) each read the old record and the later rename drops the other's entry — and a dropped entry is exactly the fail-open of the record rule above: the header-stripped team plan falls back to the file store and checkSpawn allows the spawn while another member holds the server claim. Pull: `GET /plans`, then `GET /plans/:id` for each id whose sha differs from the record, written to the read copy `<queue_state>/team/<team>/plans/<id>.md` (Background :31) unless the record maps that id to a repo path (then the local repo file is left alone and only the sha is noted for the next push's base). Push: every INDEX plan (activePlans, lib.ts:462) for which planShare (team-labels.ts:114) answers this team and whose text sha differs from the record → `PUT /plans/:id` with the record's sha as base (null when unrecorded); 409 with a sha → pull that id, keep the local file unchanged and print "team sync: <plan> conflict: the server has a newer version (<sha>); merge it and run team sync again"; 409 with `sha: null` (writePlan's answer when the base is set and the plan is absent on the server, team-server.ts:317-319) → keep the local file and print the same conflict line with no pull (a `GET /plans/:id` would 404; the record entry stays, so every later sync sends the same base and conflicts again until the plan is unshared — which drops the entry — and shared anew, whose push then carries base null). Before each push it reads the plan's holder through the remote store and skips the push when `heldByOther(holder, mainId())` (P2a's PUT has no claim check; an Unreachable holder also skips), printing "team sync: <plan> not pushed: held by another member's Main" (or "team sync: <plan> not pushed: team server <url> unreachable"); nothing is sent and the local edit stays local. A plan without `Queue: team` is never pushed (planShare returns null before the origin is read), the record rule notwithstanding. Callers: `neurastream team sync [--team <name>]` (new subcommand in src/cli/team.ts; without a caller syncTeam is dead code) runs syncTeam for each `cfg.teams` entry (or the named one); `team share` (cli/team.ts) after writing the header runs the push for that one plan (Background :32: share "adds the line and uploads the plan"); `team unshare`, after rewriting the header, deletes the plan's record entry. No hook and no heartbeat tick runs sync (each request may block its caller 5 s). Not P3: removing the plan from the server on `team unshare` (Background :32) — the server ships no `DELETE /plans/:id` (P2a has GET/PUT only); see Findings. Tests against an in-process server: personal plan never sent (fail-before: spy on the request entry); the chooser runs no git for INDEX.md, a non-plan path or a personal plan and one for a `Queue: team` plan (spy runner); record rule: a synced team plan whose header was then stripped on disk is claimed on the server at spawn and refused while login B holds it (fail-before: the header-only chooser hands it to the file store and the spawn is allowed), and a team-repo plan that was never synced and has no header sends no request; team plan synced (pull writes the read copy and the record; push sends the base sha); conflict → re-read (409 with a sha pulls, local file unchanged, the conflict line printed; 409 with `sha: null` keeps the local file, prints the conflict line and pulls nothing); the record survives two overlapping writes (two sync processes racing on one record, each writing its own entry — both entries present in record.json after both finish; the lock serializes them); `team share` pushes the plan once; summary: two team plans of one team and one personal plan in one queueSummary → exactly one `GET /claims` (spy on the request entry; fail-before: a per-plan holder sends two), and a never-synced `Queue: team` plan held on the server shows that holder in the summary, not "free" (the by-id answer); the sync one by a spy on the request entry); a claim of A's own login on main `m2` refuses A's `main` spawn with "held by Main m2" (today's text, not "another member's Main"), `claim release` without `--force` exits 1 and with `--force` frees it; a personal plan's file claim (no `mine`) with the same main is still this Main's; `list()` returns local paths, not ids; outage (server stopped, its port closed) on a team plan: holder() returns `{ unreachable: <url> }`, never null; the edit gate restores the edit with "team server <url> unreachable"; claimGate refuses the spawn with that text and leaves no claim it took, also when the outage starts between its holder() and claim() calls (a stub request entry that answers holder() and then refuses) — that case also covers the fresh-release store rule: a second team plan claimed fresh while the server was still up is released through the remote store that took it, never the file store (there the release would be a silent no-op and the claim would outlive the refusal unnoticed): no claim file appears under the claims dir, and because an unreachable release sends nothing the server claim stays until the TTL — the designed fail-closed outcome; Cites at 806e277. Built on the shipped ClaimStore (`claim`/`heartbeat`/`release`, src/core/claims.ts:39-46) and this plan's server; no second claim or lock mechanism. (0) Column helper: openTeamDb (src/core/team-server.ts:254-317) has only CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS today; there is NO ALTER and no migration in team-server.ts. This phase adds one private `addColumn(db, table, column, decl)` beside it (reads `PRAGMA table_info(<table>)`, runs `ALTER TABLE <table> ADD COLUMN <column> <decl>` only when the column is absent), called after openTeamDb's exec; P6b2 reuses it. Why it is needed: an existing database must gain columns without losing rows, and CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS never alters a table. (b) Order. Today: table `votes` (:299-303) holds one row per login whose `plan_ids` is a JSON ordered list, written by voteOf (:499-503) behind `POST /order` (:603-613, body `{ plan_ids }` checked by planIdList :526); the leader's override is one `meta` row, key `order_override`, value JSON `{ plan_ids, login, time }`, written by overrideOf (:504-509) behind `POST /order/override` (leader only through writeGate :539). Nothing sorts by either: viewPlanRows (src/core/team-client.ts:600-619) keeps the view's `ORDER BY rel_path, id` (team-server.ts:361). Change: the `votes` CREATE is replaced by `vote_ranks (login TEXT NOT NULL, plan_id TEXT NOT NULL, rank INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (rank >= 1), time INTEGER NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (login, plan_id))` (a new name, so no database keeps the old shape under it; no team server has run live yet, P8 is the first); `POST /order` takes `{ ranks: { "<plan id>": <whole number >= 1>, ... } }`, 1 to 500 entries, each key a stored plan of this team (the first bad entry named, as planIdList names it; a body without `ranks` → 400); voteOf deletes this login's vote_ranks rows and inserts the new ones in its one `.immediate` transaction and audits `POST /order` as now. The override row, overrideOf and its route stay unchanged. readTeamView (:358-382) sends `votes` as `{ login, plan_id, rank, time }[]` ordered by login, plan_id (VoteRow :331-335 takes that shape). Order rule, computed in viewPlanRows (the one sort that teamPlans, teamSummaryLines and the team page all read): a plan's score = the sum of the ranks the members who voted gave it (lower = earlier). Sort key: (1) a plan listed in the leader override comes first, in override order: an override position beats the score outright; (2) then voted plans by score ascending; (3) then plans with no vote; (4) ties on score, and the no-vote group, by `rel_path, id`. Main's "ties on score broken by the leader's override position" is covered by (1): a plan the override lists never reaches the score comparison. The team page (renderTeamPage, src/core/owner-ui.ts:1631) gains a rank form for every member (a number input `rank:<plan id>` per plan, posting to a new page route `POST /vote`; empty inputs skipped; anything but a whole number >= 1 → pageRefused 400) and, for the leader only, a form posting to `POST /override` (a number input `pos:<plan id>` per plan; empty skipped; the plans sorted by pos give `plan_ids`). Both page routes sit in pageRoute (team-server.ts:626) behind writeGate as P5's `/approve` and `/answer` do, and call voteOf/overrideOf (no copy, so they are audited there); they are `/vote` and `/override` because `/order` is the JSON route's path. (c) Pause: `neurastream team pause|resume --team <name>`; pause makes this member's Main stop claiming team plans, its held team plans get a Progress entry and are released at once (no 30-min wait); personal plans keep running. Server side exists: `POST /pause` and `/resume` (team-server.ts:614-621, pauseOf :510-515, table `paused` :304-307). The paused flag comes from `GET /view`: readTeamView gains one field, `me_paused: boolean` = a `paused` row exists for the requesting whois login (readTeamView already takes `login` for wireClaim's `mine`); isView (team-client.ts:555-566) checks it is a boolean. One CLI function in src/cli/team.ts (usage :24-25, dispatched from runTeam :43), `pauseTeam(team, cfg, paused, opts)`, does all of it: it posts `/pause` (or `/resume`) through askRaw, built as postTeamAnswer builds its write (team-client.ts:660-669); on pause it reads that team's view once and, for every view claim with `mine === true` and `main === mainId()`, appends `- <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM> <mainId>: team <name> paused by this member; claim released.` as the last line of the `## Progress` section of the plan's local file (the sync record's path, as teamPlans resolves it, team-client.ts:592-593; written by tmp + rename as claims.ts:77-79 writes), sends it with pushTeamPlan (team-client.ts:453, the same push `team share` and `team sync` use), then releases with `claimStoreFor(file, cfg).release(file, mainId())`; a held plan with no local file is only released. Resume only posts. (a) Same-goal check (Main's decision 2026-11-13: this replaces the owner's phrase "a read-only reviewer" with a deterministic check that spawns NO model; Main reports this to the owner). Owner intent kept: "what we need to be careful about is … a plan does the same thing … in a different way"; same code in different worktrees is NOT a conflict. It runs only on the SHARING member's PC, in `team share` (src/cli/team.ts:93-108), before the `Queue: team` write (:102) and the push (:107); the server never holds a forge credential or calls a forge (no keys on the server, same as model tokens). Inputs: one view read of the plan's team through the new `teamView(team, opts)` (to-do above; `GET /plans` carries no text) and the open MRs of the plan's own repo `planRepo(file)` (the one team repo whose local clone the share knows) through mr-sweep's single-repo lister `listOpenMrs` (src/core/mr-sweep.ts:251) with that member's own `glab`/`gh` login; OpenMr (:227) gains `title` (glab's `title` field and gh's already-requested `title` :215, both read in takeItems :171; "" when absent), additive, so the sweep's callers do not change. Tokens: lowercase, split on `/[^a-z0-9]+/`, drop empty and one-character tokens and these stopwords: a an the and or but of to in on for with by at from into onto is are was be been being it its this that these those as so not no we our you your they their can will should must all any each per via vs then than when where which who what how do does done make add use new. The shared plan's token set G comes from its `## Goal` (Plan.goal, the bullets parsePlan reads). parsePlan caps Plan.goal at 6 bullets (src/core/queue/lib.ts:138-139) and the check reads exactly what parsePlan returns, for the shared plan and for every view plan alike: a Goal's 7th bullet and later are never compared; stated, not changed here. Two comparisons (Main's binding choice 2026-11-13; both thresholds pinned in tests): (i) Goal vs Goal: G against the Goal token set P of every view plan whose status is not `complete` (parsePlan on its text with localPaths false); similarity = Jaccard |G∩P| / |G∪P| through the imported `jaccard` (0 when the union is empty); blocks at >= 0.5. (ii) Goal vs title: G against the title token set T of every view plan whose status is `complete` (the team's finished list: the server keeps no separate finished file, and a finished team plan stays in the view until unshared) and of every open MR; similarity = overlap coefficient |G∩T| / |T|; blocks at >= 0.6; computed only when T has >= 3 tokens after stopword removal (a shorter title is never compared and never blocks). A title of 3 or more tokens lying wholly inside a long Goal scores 1.0 and blocks: intended (Main's decision 2026-11-13; finished or open work whose whole title sits inside this Goal is the "same thing in a different way" case), and `--distinct` is the way past it. Jaccard is not used for titles: a title of m tokens against a Goal of n tokens has Jaccard <= m/n, so it could fire only when the title covers half the Goal (RevTeamQueueP6b1Haiku finding (1)). The plan's own id (`teamPlanId(remote, relPath)`) is skipped. Any item at or over its threshold blocks the share: exit 1, the file left unchanged, each overlapping item named with its score (`plan <rel_path>`, `finished <title>`, `MR <token> <title>`). An unreachable view or a failed MR listing also refuses, naming the why (fail closed). `team share <plan> --distinct "<reason>"` (reason 1 to 200 characters) skips the check and the reason goes to the server audit log: pushTeamPlan (team-client.ts:453) gains an optional `distinct` it sends in the PUT body; `PUT /plans/:id` (team-server.ts:876-918) accepts it as a string of 1 to 200 characters (else 400), and writePlan (:461-473) stores it on that PUT's audit row (its insertAudit call :471, statement :460) in a new `audit.note TEXT` column (addColumn). One new function, `goalOverlaps(goal, view, mrs, selfId)` in team-client.ts, returns the items at or over their threshold, each with its score and comparison; no new module. Config `teams[].max_share` (teams type src/core/config.ts:188; validated beside teamConfigError in src/core/team-labels.ts): optional number, 0 < x <= 1, anything else a config error naming the team; absent = no cap. No daily call counter exists in this codebase: main_call_budget shipped only the per-prompt window `mainCalls` (guards.ts:91 in EditWindow :87-94), so P6b2 reads no counter from another plan. Denominator = Σ `Run.calls` over every run `RunSource.listRuns(<local midnight today>)` returns (src/core/adapter.ts:48, the source metrics.ts:44 reads); numerator = Σ `Run.calls` over those runs whose `id` is an `agentId` in today's rows (`at` >= that midnight) of `<queue_state>/team/<name>/spawns.jsonl` (the dir is teamDir, team-client.ts:182-184, module-private at effa10d: exporting it is a to-do of this phase). A sub's Run.id is its spawn name (src/adapters/omp/sessions.ts:271; adapters/omp/index.ts:1116-1117: "tasks[].name, which is the sub's ctx.agent.id"). listRuns scans every session file of today (src/adapters/omp/sessions.ts:341-344: each jsonl at depth 1 and 2, kept by mtime), so claimGate calls it at most ONCE per gated spawn, only when at least one of the spawn's files routes to a team that sets max_share, and reuses that one result for every capped file of the spawn; claimGate runs only at the three spawn gates (taskLaunch, bypassLaunch, checkSpawn), never per tool call. spawns.jsonl is append-only (appendFileSync of one JSON line `{ planId, agentId, at }`) and only the spawn claim gate writes it. It has NO lock (no file-lock, unlike record.json), so two spawns gated at once can both read the same count and both pass before either appends: `max_share` is a SOFT cap that can overshoot under concurrent spawns; stated, not fixed. claimGate (guards.ts:1843) gains an argument `spawn: { agentIds: string[]; unnamed: boolean; runs?: RunSource }`, and the insertion point is after its per-file loop closes (:1875) and before `return null` (:1876): for each file routeTeam (team-client.ts:335, module-private at effa10d: exporting it is a to-do of this phase) sends to a team and each agentId, one line, `planId` = the stored id `teamPlanId(remote, relPath)`. Callers: taskLaunch (:1503) passes the launch's `tasks[].name` values and `unnamed` = some task has no name; bypassLaunch (:1539) passes no agentIds and `unnamed` true (a bypass command carries no task names); checkSpawn (:1931) passes `e.name` when set and `unnamed` = e.name unset. Unnamed spawns (Main's binding decision 2026-11-13): an unnamed spawn writes no spawns.jsonl row and no run id matches it, so it would bypass the cap; therefore when `unnamed` is true and any file of the spawn routes to a team that sets max_share, claimGate refuses before any claim with "team <name> sets max_share: name this agent (tasks[].name) so its calls count against the team cap", and no generated key is ever recorded. When no team of the spawn's files sets max_share, unnamed spawns stay allowed exactly as today. A multi-task launch that names several plans records every named task against every team plan of that launch (the gate sees no per-task split); stated, not fixed here. Cap check, inside the same loop before `store.claim` for each team file whose team sets max_share: denominator 0 = not capped; numerator / denominator >= max_share refuses with "team <name> cap reached: <n> of <d> calls today (max_share <x>); team plans wait until local midnight"; max_share set and no `runs` given refuses too (fail closed: the cap cannot be computed). checkTool (:1633) and checkSpawn (:1883) gain an optional trailing `runs?: RunSource`; the omp adapter passes `defaultRunSource(cfg)` at its checkTool call (adapters/omp/index.ts:1126), as it hands mainDetectors its run source (:1043); src/cli/guard.ts:55 and :71 pass none. At or over the cap this Main stops claiming team plans until the next local midnight. (e) Cross review: a team plan's work is reviewed by a Main of a different member before merge. Enforcement point: approveOf (team-server.ts:543-548 at effa10d), the one transaction both approve routes call. "Before merge" here means before the approve state flips, i.e. before approveOf inserts the approval row (:545); no server code sees a git or forge merge, so nothing else enforces it. `plans` gains `last_claimer TEXT` (addColumn; NULL = never claimed). claimOf (team-server.ts:482-502) sets it to the claiming login on both success branches (own re-claim :489-493, fresh claim :498-501) inside its transaction; its refusals (paused :484-487, held :494-497) do not set it. approveOf (today `ApprovalRow | null`, null = no such plan :544) returns a status as claimOf does: 404 no such plan; 403 when the approving login equals the plan's `last_claimer` AND the team has at least 2 members, auditing `refused <route>` as claimOf's refusals do (:485, :495); else 200 with the approval. A plan never claimed approves freely.   Findings: (1) BLOCKING line 66 writes `<queue_state>/team/<team>/record.json` by tmp + rename after each GET or PUT, and `team sync`, `team share` and `team unshare` each read-modify-write it with no lock. Two concurrent processes (two terminals, or a cron run plus a manual run) can each read the old record and the later rename drops the other's entry. That entry is the only route for a header-stripped team plan to the server (record rule, line 66 step 2): the plan then becomes a personal file claim and checkSpawn (guards.ts:1848-1862) allows the spawn while another member holds it on the server. Fix in the text: every record read-modify-write runs under the file lock the claims store already uses (claims.ts:11 `lock` from src/core/file-lock.ts), with one test that drives two overlapping record writes and asserts both entries survive; (2) non-blocking, wrong display: a team plan with `Queue: team` and no record entry on this machine (never pulled or synced here) answers "free" in queueSummary (queue/lib.ts:1259-1260) and the queue chip (owner-ui.ts:265), because line 66 says ids with no recorded path are dropped from list() and summaryHolder answers from list() by path. Decisions still ask the server per plan through teamPlanId, so no spawn is allowed; fix: summaryHolder matches list rows by id (teamPlanId per plan) instead of by recorded path; (3) non-blocking, claimGate refusal: line 66 releases this call's fresh claims "as :1858 does", but :1858 releases through the single `store` of guards.ts:1849, while line 66 has the chooser per file.
